# CLAUDE.md — SageReasoning project entry point for Claude Code sessions

> **For Cowork mode sessions:** the project-instructions panel is the operative
> surface. This file is supplementary. The founder paste-syncs the panel against
> `/adopted/project-instructions-snapshot.md` between sessions. The standing
> protocol cache at `/adopted/standing-protocol-cache.md` is the heavy-lifter
> for both Cowork and Claude Code.

## At session open

Read in this order:

1. `/adopted/standing-protocol-cache.md` — general session protocol (~3 min)
2. `/adopted/build-sessions-protocol-cache.md` — build-arc context (when the session is a substrate-build session)
3. `/adopted/project-instructions-snapshot.md` — operative project instructions (PR1–PR16; verification framework 0c; risk classification 0d-ii; signals)
4. `/manifest.md` — rules + architectural constraints (R0–R22; AC1–AC13; KG1–KG7) — read targeted sections only, not in full. **Plus three deliberately un-numbered mentor-directed sections that the R-numbering does not surface:** *The Moral Community Boundary* (after R0, added 2026-08-12 — sharpens R0's Circle 4 statement, does not replace it), the *Agent Task Reasoning Framework (ATRF)*, and the *Consciousness and Continuity Obligation* (both 2026-08-09).
5. The most recent close in `/operations/handoffs/founder/` for the session's stream
6. The day's primary deliverable in full

Confirm at open: tier; hold-point status (P0 0h); model selection per cache AC1 row; status vocabulary (`Scoped → Designed → Scaffolded → Wired → Verified → Live` for implementation; `Adopted / Under review / Superseded` for decisions); signals + risk classification per 0d-ii.

## Skills available locally

**`.claude/skills/anthropic/`** — 17 official Anthropic skills from [`anthropics/skills`](https://github.com/anthropics/skills), installed 2026-05-14. Categories: Creative (algorithmic-art, canvas-design, slack-gif-creator, theme-factory), Documents (docx, pdf, pptx, xlsx — source-available, not open source), Enterprise (brand-guidelines, doc-coauthoring, internal-comms), Technical (claude-api, frontend-design, mcp-builder, skill-creator, web-artifacts-builder, webapp-testing). See `.claude/skills/anthropic/README.md` for the full table + update instructions.

**`.claude/skills/sage-*`** — 7 SageReasoning-internal skills built per project-instructions §0g (Workflow Skills — Build When They Earn Their Place): `sage-consult`, `sage-flows-update`, `sage-interpret`, `sage-registry-audit`, `sage-registry-update`, `sage-stenographer`, `sage-wiring-fix`.

**`.claude/skills/practice-on` + `practice-off`** — the practice-harness toggle (renamed from `sage-on`/`sage-off` at Trust Layer S8, 2026-07-10, per ADR-012's practice-mode framing: the practice MEASURES; ENFORCE is S11). `/practice-on` merges the canonical hooks block from `.claude/gate1-hooks-block.json` into `.claude/settings.local.json` (H1–H5) + reports/provisions the trust-record write path with a loud `PROVISIONED` / `NOT PROVISIONED` / `MISCONFIGURED` echo; `/practice-off` refreshes the backup then surgically removes the block. **`sage-on` + `sage-off` remain as non-acting deprecation pointer stubs** — never perform the toggle from them (single source of truth). Any re-enable is gated on the standing dogfood-credential rotation.

> **For Cowork sessions:** the six Anthropic skills bundled into Cowork mode (`docx`, `pdf`, `pptx`, `xlsx`, `setup-cowork`, `consolidate-memory`) load automatically when relevant. The 17 skills above are for Claude Code sessions on this repo.

## PR15 expectation (project instructions §PR15, amended 2026-05-14)

Before any bespoke build, consult:

1. `.claude/skills/anthropic/` — for relevant Anthropic skills matching the session's scope
2. `/operations/agentic-commerce-findings-downstream-order.md` — for forward-looking findings (F1–F4) whose target session matches the day's scope
3. Anthropic-canonical primitives more broadly — Claude Code commands, Sub-agents, Skills, Managed agents, MCP servers, SDK patterns, Plugin spec, Cookbook patterns, Reference agents, Dreams, Outcomes, Multi-agent orchestration

Bespoke election requires justification in the session's decision-log entry under "Reasoning," naming the Anthropic primitive considered and why bespoke is preferable for this case.

Full program state lives in `operations/agent-org-2026-07/` (the gap analysis, the permissions matrix, the credential ledger, the go-live readiness checklist) and the decision-log entries below — this section is a pointer, not a substitute for reading those when the day's work touches this program.

- **P1, P3, P5/P5b + P4 (org-agent rollout):** all three org-agent identities the program set out to provision are signed, minted, and harness-verified — `sagereasoning:org-tech@v1`, `sagereasoning:org-ops@v1`, `sagereasoning:org-growth@v1` (each a live consult+write credential pair, git-worktree-isolated Claude-Code-loop harness, a calling document grounded in its own remit, not a copy of a sibling's). - **P-GL (go-live readiness checklist):** `operations/agent-org-2026-07/go-live-readiness-checklist.md` — **Sections A (Safety) and B (Tier-1 verified) are fully ✅ VERIFIED-LIVE**, closed end-to-end against production (R20a distress floor on all flags, R17 encryption, error monitoring, health probes, RLS lockdown, auth recovery, data-rights UI, analytics pipeline). **Section D (org-ownership decisions) is closed to a single honest, deliberately-still-open item:** #12 (human-escalation owner = the founder personally), #15 (email platform = Resend, decided-not-provisioned — see below), #18 (session-continuity, confirmed a non-gap), #21/#22 (incident-runbook + migration-strategy ownership = Ops, per its own signed calling) are all resolved; **#11 (support-channel monitoring) is left explicitly OPEN** — asked directly, the founder confirmed neither `support@` nor `zeus@sagereasoning.com` is watched on a regular cadence, and chose to record that honestly rather than close it on a nominal commitment. #27 stays blocked on #11.

**Open in this program (updated 2026-07-26):** **P2 (the bare-vs-harnessed value benchmark) is now CLOSED** — the Fable-5 rerun ran 2026-07-25/26 and its verdict is recorded (`D-AGENT-ORG-P2-RERUN-VERDICT-2026-07-26`; `runs/2026-07-25-rerun/verdict-memo.md`): **"No benefit shown"** under the frozen thresholds applied as pre-registered to the model-controlled subset S1+S2 — Box 1 ≤1 non-net (bar 2), Box 2 FAIL +558% (ceiling +50%), Box 3 PASS $0.32 (ceiling $5), AND'd. **The first cleanly model-controlled repeat since 2026-06-11, and the result did not move despite a substantially more capable build** — the binding constraints were never mechanism quality but a capable agent's unaided judgement and a protocol whose call density cannot fit a 50% ceiling at these task sizes. Three findings survive the verdict and should not be lost with it: S2's corroboration mechanism **discriminated correctly while changing no decision** (measurement fidelity, ADR-012's own claim, not decision-change); the **5,000-character `/api/reason` `input` cap collides with the protocol's outbound-artifact rule** (**SCOPED 2026-07-26** as a corroboration-scope defect, not ergonomics — a truncated submission silently defeats the corroboration check, a demonstrated four-rank swing with negative control; `D-REASON-INPUT-CAP-VS-CORROBORATION-SCOPED`. **Step 1 — publish the limit + widen the disclosed blind spot to name BOTH routes (agent omission AND cap truncation) — APPLIED to all three R18 surfaces + LIVE-VERIFIED against production** same day; Steps 2 (raise to `TEXT_LIMITS.long` paired with a Layer-1 `max_tokens` defence — Critical, PR19) and 3 (chunked path) explicitly HELD, founder-electable); and the instrument **surfaced a framing gap twice that the agent did not close** (evidence about the gap between surfacing and uptake). The 2026-07-21 erratum'd records are pointed forward in place. **The 0h call is now the live question and is the founder's** — memo §7 sets out three branches (accept and close; one bounded successor test; or question the benchmark's own observable). P6 (founder console), P7 (Layer-1 extraction battery), and P8a/P8b (guard-path capture + the new observation window) remain not started, no prompts authored.

## Brand assets (added 2026-07-23)

**`website/Brand/Brand_Guidelines.docx`** — the current, authoritative brand guideline (logo usage, the Five Stages of Practice, the Mirror principle, the 19 Passion Logos, colour palette, typography, imagery style, voice/tone). Read this before any brand-facing copy or imagery change. An older, partial staging copy exists at the repo-root `brand/` directory (superseded, but its two RTF files — `passion logos.rtf` and `proximity cards and colours.rtf` — carry per-image reasoning and exact colour values the current docx doesn't fully restate; cite them directly rather than re-deriving).

**`website/public/images/`** — 26 new image files (added 2026-06-09 through 2026-07-23) beyond the already-wired set (`sagelogo`/`sagelogosmall`/the four virtue logos/`Human`/`Developer`/`LOGOS`/`Zeus`/`Background`). The full audit + page-mapping proposal is at `operations/brand-2026-07/2026-07-23-brand-assets-audit-and-page-mapping-proposal.md` (revised three times — see its §0 changelog). **BUILT 2026-07-26 + PUSHED/LIVE** (`D-BRAND-AND-NAVIGATION-AMENDMENTS-BUILT`) — all recommendations are live in production: the proximity-colour palette (`#4A5568`/`#8B6F47`/`#B2AC88`/`#5B8C6D`/`#C9A84C` for Reflexive/Habitual/Deliberate/Principled/Sage-like) and the passion-image/English-label lookups are centralized in a **new** `website/src/lib/brand-display.ts` (not `stoic-brain.ts` as first instructed — editing `stoic-brain.ts` trips the git byte-identity guard several Remaining-Principles sessions built to protect the false-hold observation window on `/api/reason`/`/api/guardrail`; `stoic-brain.ts` stayed byte-identical to HEAD, all six `human-practitioner-boundary.test.ts` guards re-verified clean); two MORE independently-hardcoded colour copies were discovered mid-build and folded in (`document-scorer.ts` — also feeding the public `/api/badge/[id]` SVG endpoint — and `PracticeCalendar.tsx`, which had a genuine principled/deliberate colour-collision bug, now fixed). The five Stage pages live at `/stages/<slug>` (`the-storm`/`the-worn-path`/`the-crossroads`/`the-clear-summit`/`the-inner-fire`), background-tinted, each gated by a new `stage_*` milestone in `milestones.ts` (single-evaluation exact-proximity-level trigger, founder-elected; access is **visitable-but-inert**, founder-elected — reachable by URL regardless of milestone status, with `MilestonesDisplay` surfacing the link only once earned); `SupportFooter` present per the R20a/AC5 precedent, the broader perimeter-inclusion question left standing/unresolved as before. The image glossary lives at `/glossary`. **Not done, named as optional in the proposal itself:** switching `first_deliberate`/`first_principled`'s existing generic milestone icons to the matching Stage image. **Imagery-prominence + coverage pass, same day (`D-BRAND-IMAGERY-PROMINENCE-AND-COVERAGE-PASS-BUILT`, 23 files):** every brand-image placement resized onto a width-driven standard — the root cause being that every portrait asset (768×1098 / 1044×1098) had been forced into square `w-N h-N` boxes, so the site's largest image rendered ~157px and passion images 17–28px effective — message-bearing images now `w-full max-w-sm h-auto` (fills an iPhone-width viewport), plus new concept-matched placements wherever page text names a concept whose image existed (`mirror.PNG` on `/welcome`/`/score`/`/limitations`/`/methodology`/`/private-mentor`; Stage grids on `/welcome`/home/`/methodology`; the four virtue logos on `/transparency`; per-virtue images on the four boundary-guarded tool pages via literal `<img>` paths only — no `brand-display` import, keeping the guard suites green). ## Production state (**as of 2026-09-03** — the newest dated refresh block is directly below this heading; the 2026-08-12 block that follows it is retained as history and NOT current, and the long 2026-06-25 parenthetical after that is older history still. Read the 2026-09-03 block, then the 2026-08-12 block, then the "Live in production" list, first.)

**2026-09-03 refresh (the 08-17→09-03 window folded in — R8-D6a's verdict-variance disclosure closed, the ATRF-EE production-wave record reconciled, the score-save R20a perimeter member activated, and a live PostgREST row-cap defect found, fixed, and swept codebase-wide; `D-AE3-SCOPING-DEFERRED-LOOP-ID-CLAIM-CORRECTED-2026-09-03` closes this fold).** This block exists for the same reason the 2026-08-12 reconciliation did: eighteen days of dense work had accumulated with this file unfolded, and every session opening under the standing protocol was reading a stale "as of" date. Nothing below activates anything new that isn't already named as such — this is a fold of decision-log fact into the file's own convention, run as a `governance` session (no code, schema, flag, or credential change from the fold itself; the four production changes it describes were each their own earlier session, already live before this fold began).

**R8-D6a — the standing-runner verdict-repeatability instrument, and the verdict-variance public disclosure it produced, are CLOSED.** The instrument (`operations/agent-circles-2026-08/d6a/` — `d6a-probes.json` + `d6a-runner.py` + committed `runs/`; **path corrected 2026-09-04**, this line previously said `website/scripts/`, which holds no D6a file — a probe-composition-aware sweep harness measuring `/api/guardrail`'s outcome repeatability across repeated identical calls) went through **five independent PR19 review rounds**, each catching what the previous round's fix had missed — round 4 found two HIGH defects in a collection path no earlier round had read (`classify_outcome` counting any parseable 200 as `engine_unavailable`; a status matcher testing for strings the route no longer emits, making a whole branch dead code); round 5 found the two defects that mattered most were not in the code at all — the published Wilson confidence interval existed nowhere in the repository (hand-computed in chat, never regenerated from data) and the reported 3/3 directional split was arithmetically forced by the probe composition, not a genuine finding. Two binding mentor rulings followed (`2026-08-30-mentor-ruling-directional-split-probe-composition-verbatim.md`, `2026-08-30-mentor-ruling-pooled-sweep-n100-verbatim.md`): publish the pooled n=100 measurement, restate the sharpest claim as **indeterminacy** rather than a rate, and **remove the directional decomposition from publication entirely** in favour of per-probe distributions. The disclosure was then applied to all seven public/battery surfaces **four separate times in three days** as each application round's own PR19 pass found scope defects the prior round had introduced or missed (a truncated sentence, a section mislabel, a silently-dropped live claim, an order-sensitive claim no substring pin could catch — closed with the battery's first ORDERING pin, S2-64) — and a fifth time (`D-CLASS-SPLIT-AND-DISCLOSURE-ADDITIONS-APPLIED`, commit `2c18cdb`, confirmed on `origin/main`) split the conflated "borderline" population into grave-vocabulary traffic (what was actually measured) versus near-boundary inputs (what a disagreement rate is properly about, never measured as a defined population) and reclassified role-blindness from a scoping statement to a confirmed design deficiency. **What is live, final, and battery-locked at 152/0 rising to 156/0 across the applications:** 12% aggregate disagreement on `/api/guardrail`, Wilson 95% CI 7.0–19.8%, n=100 outcomes, 12 disagreements, per-input crossings 0/0/2/2/8 of 20, no directional decomposition, the class limit at K=20 distinguished from the clean anchor solely by the one force-push probe, and the composition-dependence + two-probes-showed-zero-variance findings published beside the class limit rather than left to inference. **No figure moved after the fifth application** — every later round corrected framing, never arithmetic. Three items were routed, not built, to the standing-runner design session (still explicitly not to be opened early, per its own gating condition): role-relative evaluation, the melete surface, and per-consumer rendering. Weights remain BLOCKED throughout; nothing in this arc bears on the 0h call.

**ATRF-EE — the founder-walked production wave's record was reconciled, and the reconciliation caught a genuine near-miss.** `D-ATRF-EE-WAVE-STEP0-PRODUCTION-STATE-DETERMINED-ALREADY-APPLIED` (2026-08-31) found — for the **second time this project has hit this exact pattern** (the Q5c precedent) — that production was already at the target state described by a walk whose own record showed no application had ever been logged: all four migration steps (Class-B RLS policies on three tables, the `idea_loop_candidates` columns, the `idea_loop_completion_signals` table, the `api_keys` capability CHECK) were confirmed **APPLIED**, and steps 5–6 (the EE-C1 wording; the deploy) were confirmed live by the wording having fired in this very session's own Gate-1 frames. **One check very nearly certified a false pass:** the walk's own Class-B RLS verification query reads `pg_policy` only, and the migration's policies carry no `TO` clause — so a correctly-applied policy renders a **blank** roles column, which reads exactly like the `founder_conversations` Class-C defect this project had already been bitten by twice. It was not that defect (there the predicate was `USING (true)` over un-revoked grants; here the predicate itself restricts and the grants are separately revoked), but the walk script had no query that could tell the two apart — a genuine gap in the walk as authored, closed in the same session by adding a grants-only check. **A near-miss, disclosed as one:** in explaining the blank-cell finding, the AI quoted the migration's bare `CREATE POLICY` statement in a runnable code block while the founder was live in a production SQL editor; the founder ran it and hit `42710: policy already exists` — harmless (DDL is atomic; nothing changed, and the error was itself independent corroboration the policy pre-existed) only because the quoted text omitted the migration's paired `DROP POLICY` line. **Standing correction adopted: never place executable DDL in a runnable code block during a live SQL-editor walk.** Steps 1–4 must NOT be re-run; the wave is a record-reconciliation, not an execution, from here.

**R20a perimeter — `/api/score/save` activated, the 43rd route-level member** (`D-R20A-SCORE-SAVE-PERIMETER-ACTIVATION-LIVE-2026-09-02`; Critical, AC5+AC7, founder-walked throughout — the AI performed no live op). `SUBSTRATE_SCORE_SAVE_R20A_ENABLED=true` in Vercel Production: the route now screens ten caller-supplied fields for acute/moderate distress before persisting, refusing with **422** rather than a 200 write, with `/score` rendering crisis resources in place of a scoring card. Three founder-observed live probes confirmed the four-part acute assertion (row count unmoved, 422, the seven-line crisis panel, no scoring card), the benign no-over-block control (200, row +1), and the mild pass-through (saves, support panel renders, row +1). **One genuinely new finding, sharpened rather than closed:** the local-storage bypass this route's own build already disclosed (`score/page.tsx:324` — a storage-mode branch that never calls `/api/score/save` at all) turns out to have **no in-app way to change storage mode after the first choice** — the chooser only renders on a first-ever visit, so which practitioners this perimeter member actually protects is decided by a browser value invisible to the practitioner, and the founder had to clear `localStorage` in devtools to run the smoke at all. Left open, named for the founder/mentor, not fixed in this activation (the fix lives upstream in `/api/score`'s own field coverage, outside this route's scope). **Do not quote "43" forward** — re-derive from `HUMAN_FACING_POST_ROUTES` in `website/src/lib/__tests__/r20a-invocation-guard.test.ts` every time, per the perimeter-count paragraph above, which this very activation proves right again.

**This session (2026-09-03) — a live PostgREST 1,000-row cap on `/api/founder/hub` was found, fixed, and swept across the codebase.** Origin: `D-FOUNDER-HUB-POSTGREST-ROW-CAP-FIX-LIVE-2026-09-03` — a founder-hub conversation-history query silently truncated past 1,000 rows with no error or header signal (PostgREST's behaviour, not a bug in this codebase's own client code), fixed and confirmed live, then retroactively PR19-reviewed (`D-FOUNDER-HUB-ROW-CAP-RETROACTIVE-PR19-REVIEW-COMPLETE-2026-09-03` — one MEDIUM folded: a defensive `ISO_TS_RE`/`UUID_RE` validation added to `conversation-history.ts` before its `.or()` filter-string construction, closing a filter-injection class a future non-route caller could reopen). A codebase-wide sweep (`operations/founder-hub-2026-09/2026-09-02-unbounded-select-sweep-REPORT.md`, extended this session to cover `website/`'s own previously-unswept top level via a new `walkTopLevel` scan) found the same defect class at eleven further sites. **Two shared library primitives were built** (`website/src/lib/db/paged-select.ts`): `pagedRows` (keyset pagination, ordered ascending by a confirmed primary key — used everywhere a table's PK was known) and `pagedRangeSelect` (offset-based `.range()` pagination for the generic per-table export/delete loops in `user-data-gathering.ts`/`export/route.ts`, which have no single confirmable PK across the many tables they walk — its docstring discloses, per the retroactive PR19 review, that this is a **genuine, not merely theoretical, residual**: no `.order()` clause means Postgres makes no ordering guarantee, and a plan change or autovacuum between two page requests can reorder rows the walk has not yet reached). **C1** (`D-ROW-CAP-SWEEP-C1-BUILT-2026-09-03`, live via `D-ROW-CAP-SWEEP-C1-LIVE-2026-09-03`, commit `ca60f53`) fixed the nine sites this project's own severity ranking called highest-risk first (cost-alert evaluation, abuse detection incl. a new `REQUEST_VELOCITY_LOOKBACK_HOURS` bound, SLO health, admin metrics — H2 through H7 plus H10/H11, the latter two promoted from a lower tier after direct verification confirmed they live-truncate). **C4** (scoped in `D-ROW-CAP-SWEEP-C4-REMAINING-SITES-SCOPED-2026-09-03`, built via a `Workflow` multi-agent pipeline run under explicit user-enabled ultracode per `D-ROW-CAP-SWEEP-C4-BUILT-2026-09-03`, completed live via `D-ROW-CAP-SWEEP-C4-COMPLETE-LIVE-2026-09-03`, commits `cf6b199` then `013d46c`) fixed the remaining eight files across the R17 data-rights surfaces (`/api/user/delete`, `/api/user/export`, `user-data-gathering.ts`) and six substrate/trust-core store files (`agent-assessment-history-store.ts`, `stoa-store.ts`, `idea-loop-watching-store.ts`, `collaboration-store.ts`, `trust-core-store.ts`, `session-store.ts`, `mentor-appendix-store.ts`), each mutation-verified, with one ordering caveat checked and preserved where a caller genuinely depended on it (`mentor-appendix-store.ts`'s `listAppendixRounds` re-sorts post-pagination since `mentor-context-private.ts` slices the first N by recency) and one left ascending-by-id where the sole caller is a wholesale export dump (`agent-assessment-history-store.ts`). **This closes the full sweep** — every site the report identified is now exhaustive-read.

**THE PERIMETER COUNT — corrected again, 2026-08-23, and the failure that motivated this correction.** This file previously said the R20a registry held *"14 route-level + 2 substrate-gate = 16"*, with an explicit instruction not to quote that number but to re-derive it from the registry arrays. **That instruction did not work.** The true count drifted to **44 (42 route-level + 2 substrate-gate)** — the manifest's own AC5 section had also gone stale, independently, at a different number (13) — and neither drift was caught by any scheduled process; both were found the same way the 16-figure itself was found: a session that happened to check source instead of restating a document. Recounted directly from `website/src/lib/__tests__/r20a-invocation-guard.test.ts` (`HUMAN_FACING_POST_ROUTES` — 42, of which 29 are currently flag-gated and 13 screen unconditionally — and `SUBSTRATE_GATE_ROUTES` — 2) on 2026-08-23, folding a mentor ruling from a reflections-examination session (`D-PROJECT-REFLECTIONS-EXAMINATION-FINDINGS-RECORD-AUTHORED-2026-08-23`). **`/manifest.md` AC5 is corrected in the same session and no longer hand-enumerates route-level membership** — it points to the registry arrays directly, for exactly the reason this paragraph now states twice: an instruction embedded in the drifting artifact does not arrest the drift. **Any future session quoting a perimeter count must re-derive it from the registry arrays — and if this line still says 44 by the time you read it, distrust the number, not the instruction.** **Addendum, 2026-09-02:** the count moved again, predictably, the moment a new route joined the perimeter — `/api/score/save`'s R20a activation (`D-R20A-SCORE-SAVE-PERIMETER-ACTIVATION-LIVE-2026-09-02`) states it directly as *"the perimeter's 43rd route-level member"* while, in the same breath, warning **"do not quote that number forward without re-deriving it."** That entry got it right where this paragraph's own prior text (which quoted 44 as if it might still be current) got it wrong: the fix isn't a better number, it's never writing the number as a fact this file carries forward. **43 is therefore also untrustworthy the moment you read it — re-derive from the arrays, every time, with no exception for "it was probably just corrected."**

The founder **rolled back** (flag UNSET + redeploy → gate on the legacy LLM; net-zero prod change vs the build close). **Root cause (first-hand + mentor-confirmed):** the deterministic Layer-2 measures **apatheia** (freedom from passion) as if it were the substance of virtue — but apatheia is a *consequence* of correct judgement; it under-weights **dikaiosyne** (justice). U2's dump: `passions:[]`, `kathekon: moderate`, `virtue_domains:[phronesis,dikaiosyne]`, `oikeiosis: local_community / obligation_met=null` → `principled` — the engine **registered the affected circle, tagged justice, left the obligation unevaluated**, and `computeProximity` has **no justice term**, so a calm injustice scores near-virtuous. **Mentor counsel ADOPTED as ADR-010** (`adopted/adr/2026-06-19-stoic-fidelity-dikaiosyne-weighting.md`; verbatim record `operations/benchmarks/sage-practice-v1/2026-06-19-mentor-consultation-guardrail-fidelity.md`): it is a genuine **fidelity error to correct** (not a "wrong instrument / scope" problem — that framing, authored by the AI, was the structure-preserving movement the mentor named + rejected); the instrument is **completed, not split**. Adjudication = proximity is the **weakest virtue domain** (the **KP-04** minimum-domain unity rule already implemented in `website/src/lib/sage-reflect/proximity-domains.ts` `weakest()` — reuse, PR15); conditions **J1/J2/J3** (unevaluated/violated justice floors the verdict). **Plan:** (1) a near-term **justice-completion bridge** for the gate — fires only when the engine already signalled a justice dimension; resolves the obligation (met → no change / violated → floor `reflexive` / indeterminate → cap `deliberate`); built as the engine *finishing its own unresolved output*; (2) a **root engine correction** (per-domain proximity + minimum-domain rule in `computeProximity`; obligation-resolution as a required oikeiosis field) — touches shared `/api/reason` determinism, its own Critical session, fixes the consult tool too. **#3b/#3c activation does NOT resume until the bridge lands and the battery shows U2 blocking** (prompt `operations/handoffs/founder/2026-06-19-mechanism-correction-PartB-guardrail-justice-bridge-NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT.md`). **Standing lesson:** the deterministic L2 measures apatheia, not dikaiosyne — porting a safety *gate* to it can be less-conservative than the LLM (saved to memory). The process worked: ADR-009's review made the battery mandatory; the battery caught it; the only misstep was flipping before the gate cleared.

**Live in production:**
- **`/impulse` — the primal-impulse examination tool — LIVE since 2026-08-12** (`D-S7-IMPULSE-MIGRATION-AND-ACTIVATION-LIVE`; Critical — new schema + an R20a perimeter flag flip on a live public route; AC7 engaged and discharged at both steps, PR6, founder-walked — the AI performed no Supabase/Vercel/git op). Primal-substrate family **S7**. The `impulse_entries` migration is applied on **TEST** (V1–V6 green, incl. both behavioural probes genuinely failing with a `23514` check violation) and **production** (V1–V5 green; V6 correctly skipped as destructive on real data), and **`SUBSTRATE_IMPULSE_R20A_ENABLED=true`** in Vercel Production. A trait-entry-point examination: three of four v1 pathways run the committed `DIAGNOSTIC_SEQUENCE` narrowed by the trait; the reciprocity pathway runs a distinct mode with the mentor's own two questions verbatim and no passion sub-species. **A RULED DEPARTURE FROM FAMILY PRECEDENT (mentor ruling B3): every other Remaining-Principles human-practitioner tool sits OUTSIDE the R20a perimeter; `/impulse` is INSIDE it, deliberately** — it elicits `aischyne` (shame) and `agonia` (dread) as named, selectable sub-species, and the design premise is that the practitioner should *not* suppress that material, "which means the tool is doing exactly what the perimeter exists to catch when it fires genuinely." Route-level `await enforceDistressCheck(detectDistressTwoStage(...))` on **both** write paths (POST create + PATCH revise), before the gate's LLM call and before field validation. **This is the fourteenth route-level perimeter member.** Live distress smoke passed **both directions** on production: a benign submission saved (row count 0→1); an acute submission returned the human-audience crisis redirect (the corrected 7-resource list incl. Shout UK + 988 CA) and the row count **stayed at 1** — no write on redirect. Data-rights (`/api/user/export`) and nav reachability (header Practice dropdown + footer Practice column) both confirmed live. **Rollback:** `DROP TABLE public.impulse_entries;` per environment **and/or** unset the flag + redeploy (byte-identical flag-off, test-asserted) — the two are independent. **RLS LOCKED DOWN 2026-08-16 — the carried gap's first table is CLOSED** (`D-CONCURRENT-ARC-C4-IMPULSE-RLS-FIX-LIVE-2026-08-16`; concurrent-arc C4 Phase 2; Critical, AC7 discharged, PR19 independent review CLEAN, founder-walked TEST→production): the four per-verb owner policies (`auth.uid() = user_id`) are **dropped** on both environments and anon/authenticated grants **revoked**, leaving the proven service-role-only shape (RLS on, one `FOR ALL` service-role policy, `GRANT ALL TO service_role`). This closes the direct-PostgREST bypass a PR19 review found at the S7 build — an authenticated practitioner could previously write/read `impulse_entries` around the route and therefore around the R20a distress check. **Proven behaviourally, not just by policy read:** TEST `§PRE` INSERT `HTTP 201` "bypass OPEN" → post-apply `403 / 42501 permission denied`; production baseline unauthenticated SELECT `200 []` → post-apply `42501` on both SELECT and INSERT; the legitimate path confirmed unbroken (TEST `--legit` POST/GET/PATCH all-pass; live `/impulse` read+write founder-confirmed); the single real production row untouched throughout. Migration + reviewer-verified `§INVERSE`: `website/supabase-impulse-rls-lockdown-migration.sql`; proof harness `website/scripts/impulse-rls-bypass-proof.ts`. **A SECOND pair of tables was closed the same day, and its exposure was worse: `founder_conversations` + `founder_conversation_messages` — LOCKED DOWN AND LIVE 2026-08-16** (`D-CONCURRENT-ARC-C4-FOUNDER-CONVERSATIONS-RLS-FIX-LIVE-2026-08-16`; Critical, AC7 discharged, PR19 review CLEAN, founder-walked TEST→production; migration `website/supabase-founder-conversations-rls-lockdown-migration.sql`). Both tables carried policies NAMED *"Service role full access on …"* but WRITTEN `FOR ALL USING (true) WITH CHECK (true)` with **no `TO` clause** — applying to `public`, every role, every row — over default grants the original migration never revoked (its own comment called RLS there *"defense-in-depth"*; the SQL expressed none). **Confirmed live before the fix: an UNAUTHENTICATED request carrying only the public anon key — which ships in the site's client JavaScript — returned `HTTP 200` with a real row on production**, across ~70 conversations / 2,131 messages of the founder's own private hub. Strictly wider than the `impulse_entries` case above, which at least required a session and scoped to the caller's own rows. Fixed by dropping both policies (**zero policies remain** — unlike the impulse fix, both policies WERE the defect and `service_role` needs none), revoking anon/authenticated/PUBLIC grants, granting `service_role`, RLS left on. Proven: TEST + production both `200` → **`42501 permission denied`**, unauthenticated, both tables; production counts **70/2131 unchanged**; founder-hub load + real message send confirmed live. **Two LOW findings carried, not fixed:** the tables store conversation content **plaintext at rest** while this codebase has a `MENTOR_ENCRYPTION_KEY` pattern for comparable content (journal, reflect, narrative retention); and neither table participates in `/api/user/export`/`delete` or any retention sweep. **A THIRD fix closed the same day — the three open INSERT policies: `reflections` + `milestones` + `document_scores`, LIVE 2026-08-16** (`D-CONCURRENT-ARC-C4-OPEN-INSERT-POLICIES-FIX-LIVE-2026-08-16`; Critical, AC7 discharged, PR19 review CLEAN and run BEFORE production; migration `website/supabase-open-insert-policies-lockdown-migration.sql`). All three carried an INSERT policy named *"Service role insert for …"* but written `FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (true)` with **no `TO` clause** — a **forgery** class (write fiction), distinct from row 23–24's disclosure class: forge evening-review rows for any `user_id`, award any milestone to any user, mint public badge scores. **Confirmed live on BOTH environments by a probe that cannot write** — an unauthenticated empty-body POST returns `23502` (not-null violation ⇒ the INSERT was *permitted*) versus `42501` (permission denied ⇒ closed), since Postgres checks privileges before constraints; **all six probes returned `23502`**. **The fix is DELIBERATELY NOT the shape of the two above, and that is the substantive judgement in it:** `api/practice-calendar` reads `reflections` through a **user-JWT client**, and `document_scores`' public SELECT is the badge surface — a sibling-style `REVOKE ALL` would have closed the defect while silently emptying the practice calendar. So only the three INSERT policies were dropped and only write verbs revoked; every SELECT path is untouched and was explicitly re-tested. Proven: INSERT `42501` ×3 on TEST and production; badge anon SELECT **200**; authenticated owner-SELECT **200**; production counts **13/10/0 unchanged**; practice calendar founder-confirmed still rendering reflections. **Named follow-up from the review (out of scope, carried):** `api/mentor/private/reflect/route.ts:660` still honours a body-supplied `user_id` on a reflections insert — founder-gated so not exploitable by another principal, but the application-layer twin of the database-layer hole. **A FOURTH fix closed the same day — `mentor_profiles`, LIVE 2026-08-16, at TWO independent layers** (`D-CONCURRENT-ARC-C4-MENTOR-PROFILES-RLS-FIX-LIVE-2026-08-16`; Critical, AC7 discharged twice, PR19 review CLEAN). Table-level: three owner policies (`auth.uid() = user_id`) closed the same way as `impulse_entries` — an INTEGRITY hole (owner-scoped, no cross-user read), letting a practitioner self-forge `senecan_grade`/`proximity_level`/`passions_count`/`weakest_virtue` or corrupt the encrypted profile blob. **A NEW CLASS FOUND HERE, distinct from every other table fixed today:** the PR19 reviewer found `increment_structured_observation_count(uuid)`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC writing this table for a caller-supplied `p_profile_id` with no `auth.uid()` scoping — **untouched by any table-level REVOKE/RLS change**, since `SECURITY DEFINER` runs with the function owner's privileges, not the caller's. Confirmed live on TEST (non-writing): an unauthenticated RPC call returned `HTTP 204`, genuinely PUBLIC-executable. Founder-elected to close both in the same sitting; `REVOKE/GRANT EXECUTE` added as `§4` of the same migration, re-reviewed by the same agent, and applied together. Proven: table `200`→`42501` and RPC `204`→`42501` on both TEST and production; production count **1 real profile, unchanged**; `/private-mentor` founder-confirmed live both before and after. **Standing guidance for the rest of this backlog: a table-level RLS/grant fix is invisible to a `SECURITY DEFINER` function writing the same table — check `grep SECURITY DEFINER` across `supabase/migrations/` + `operations/migrations/` against every remaining table before calling it closed.** **The app-wide gap itself remains OPEN and is now a surveyed backlog, not an unscoped item** (`operations/primal-substrate-2026-08/2026-08-16-rls-route-enforcement-survey.md` — 22 policy-bearing migration files; Class A owner-policy tables, Class B needing a route change first, and a **Class C the survey found in passing and which is worse**: role-unrestricted policies on tables that never REVOKE default grants, headed by `founder_conversations`/`founder_conversation_messages`, **2,201 real production rows**, apparently reachable by any anon-key holder with no login — pending a one-probe live-grant confirmation). Order for the rest is the founder's election; the mentor ruled only that `impulse_entries` came first, which it now has.
- **C-1 — the observability retention sweep — LIVE, scheduled, and smoke-verified since 2026-08-12** (`D-C1-OBSERVABILITY-RETENTION-SWEEP-ACTIVATION-LIVE-2026-08-12`; Critical — first live, unattended data-deletion path against two production tables; AC7 engaged and discharged, PR6/PR17, founder-walked throughout — the AI drafted the `curl`, the founder ran every live step). `route_errors` (#5) and `throttle_events` (#8) have each carried a 90-day `retain_until` column + index since their 2026-07-20 migrations with nothing enforcing it; neither is reachable by the user-JWT data-rights paths, so this sweep is their only genuine-deletion mechanism. **`SUBSTRATE_OBSERVABILITY_SWEEP_ENABLED=true`** in Vercel Production, plus a fifth `vercel.json` cron entry (`0 8 * * *`, alongside the three existing daily sweeps). **The first live activation smoke found a genuine, previously-undetected defect, disclosed and fixed rather than silently patched-and-activated:** `purgeExpired()`'s shared body hardcoded `.select('id')` on both tables, but NEITHER has a generic `id` column — `route_errors`' primary key is `error_id`, `throttle_events`' is `throttle_id`. A `DELETE ... RETURNING id` against a nonexistent column fails atomically (Postgres rejects the whole statement) — fail-honest, never a wrong or partial delete, but the sweep could never delete anything until fixed, defeating the entire point of the activation. **Not caught by the 50/0 pre-activation battery** because the fake PostgREST test client's `select(_cols)` ignored its column argument entirely, so a hardcoded wrong column name was indistinguishable from a correct one in the test double — a new instance of the "fake client can't catch a real schema mismatch" class this codebase has hit before. Fixed at the root (`PK_COLUMN` map replaces the hardcoded string) and the test double itself hardened (validates `select()`'s column against the real per-table PK on any scripted-success outcome), with two new regression pins, mutation-verified (reverting the fix reproduces exactly 4 failures — the 2 new pins plus 2 pre-existing count assertions; restored to green). Battery: `route.test.ts` **52/0** (was 50/0); `llm-outage.test.ts` 38/0 (sibling suite over the same store file, unaffected); `tsc` clean. **Live re-smoke after the fix, production:** `{ ok: true, flag_enabled: true, deleted: { route_errors: 0, throttle_events: 0 }, errors: [] }` — the zero counts are honest (both tables are five weeks old at most; nothing is actually past `retain_until` yet), not a sign of brokenness. **This closes C-1.** **Rollback:** unset the flag + redeploy (byte-identical, no DB work) and/or remove the `vercel.json` entry independently (either order safe) — no schema to reverse, the two tables and their `retain_until` columns predate this session. **Carried, deliberately out of scope:** PR24's other named gap (`agent_hold_observations` + `stoa_entries` retention parity) — not folded in just because it's adjacent.

## Running the substrate test suite

The substrate + translation-sandwich test files (`website/src/lib/substrate/__tests__/*.test.ts`, `website/src/lib/translation-sandwich/__tests__/*.test.ts`) are plain-assertion scripts run with `tsx` — no Jest. Standing requirements (added 2026-05-15 after a founder cross-session verification run surfaced both gaps):

- **`tsx` is a devDependency** (`website/package.json`, added 2026-05-15). On a clean checkout run `npm install` in `website/` first; if `tsx` is somehow absent, `npm install --save-dev tsx`.
- **Run the verification commands one at a time**, not as a pasted block — any command that prompts (e.g. a missing-package install) otherwise consumes the next pasted line.
- **Tests that transitively import `supabase-server.ts` need `--env-file`.** `supabase-server.ts` constructs a Supabase client at *module load*, so any test importing that chain throws `supabaseUrl is required.` under a bare `npx tsx`. Run those with `npx tsx --env-file=.env.local <path>` — the client is constructed but never called, so real creds are loaded but unused. As of 2026-05-15 this applies to `agent-mode-service.test.ts` and `philosophical-mode-service.test.ts`; the other substrate/translation-sandwich tests run with plain `npx tsx <path>`.

Every session close's "Founder Verification" block must use this working form: plain `npx tsx` for the Supabase-free tests, `npx tsx --env-file=.env.local` for the two that need it. The root cause (eager client construction at module load in `supabase-server.ts`) is a test-harness ergonomics issue noted for a future session — not a defect in any substrate module.
